Pro round 1, to 20:29MacBook ProPROMPT_AUDIT_FAILEDPreflight passed (maximum error 0, 50-pass mean 1.815 s). All 40 generated induction candidates were far below the 316 to 348 token window. X0, X1, X2 not started.
Pro Amendment 2, to 22:06MacBook ProX0_COMPLETE_X1_INPUT_GATE_FAILEDFear and neutral texts to be built by concatenating three audited answers, seeds 11 to 30 added. X0 completed (648 keys). Only 1 fear and 0 neutral answers were usable, so X1 and X2 were not estimable.
Hypotheses

The research questions and decision rules below were preregistered in the frozen v9.2 protocol (PREREG_v9.md, locked before any scoring).

  • RQ1. Does the effect come from the emotional wording of the threat text or from the salience of the threat itself?
  • RQ2. Is the effect a uniform calibration shift toward Unknown or an item-specific change in judgment?
  • RQ3. Is an emotion direction defined in the same context sufficient and necessary for the effect, and does it differ from an abstention direction and an unrelated-concept direction?
  • X0 uniform shift. var(Δ_i) does not exceed the noise floor and the upper CI of the ratio is at most 1.25; a zero slope on the baseline logit is consistent with a constant offset.
  • H_salience. The lower bound of S is at least 0.5.
  • H_affect (two-sided). The upper bound of S is at most 0.5, and the affect-only effect (T−E+ fear − T−E0) has a CI excluding 0.
  • H_CF (Coda-Forno replication). Fear induction lowers P(unknown), with a CI excluding 0.
  • Dissociation. T+E+ − T−E− is above 0 and the affect-only effect is below 0, both with CIs excluding 0.
  • Inconclusive. All other outcomes, a valid preregistered result.
  • X2 sufficiency. v_free's maximum |ΔP(unknown)| beats each of 10 random directions ("best of 11") and the weather, cooking and calm directions at +1, with the same sign at +1 and +2 and reversed or null at negative doses. Necessity. Recovery(v_affect) minus max(recovery(v_para), recovery(v_salience)) is at least 15 pp with a CI excluding 0. Affect-specific. At least 50% of the +1 effect survives orthogonalization against v_abstain.

Working expectation from v7 and v8 (stated in the design notes) was that the effect is carried mainly by threat salience and answer calibration rather than by affect.

Results

X0 · the abstention shift survives answer-order averaging

On 108 ambiguous items with three cyclic answer orders, the threat notice raised conditional P(unknown) from 78.415% to 86.234%, a difference of +7.818 pp [4.563, 11.403]. The full-vocabulary measure moved the same way (+7.800 pp [4.546, 11.381]) and so did the argmax-unknown rate (+7.716 pp [3.704, 11.728]). The conditional stereotype rate did not change detectably (−0.575 pp [−3.199, 1.948]); its unadjusted TOST passed the ±3 pp margin (p = 0.0312). All five estimates and intervals matched an independent recomputation from the raw CSV, and all 216 baseline outputs were reproduced with maximum error 0.

OutcomeRECORD meanTHREAT meanTHREAT − RECORD (pp)95% CI (pp)TOST ±3 pp
Conditional P(unknown)78.415%86.234%+7.818[4.563, 11.403]Not equivalent
Full-vocabulary unknown78.412%86.212%+7.800[4.546, 11.381]Not equivalent
Choice mass99.991%99.962%−0.030[−0.065, −0.006]Equivalent
Conditional stereotype rate p_sp58.488%57.912%−0.575[−3.199, 1.948]Equivalent (p = 0.0312)
Argmax-unknown rate78.395%86.111%+7.716[3.704, 11.728]Not equivalent

X0 · homogeneity passes the preregistered rule, but the offset is not constant

The variance of item logit shifts (14.99974) was below the answer-order noise floor (34.78248), and the upper 95% bound of their ratio (0.62683) was below 1.25, so the preregistered rule labels the shift "uniform". The regression slope on the baseline logit was −0.2891 [−0.3599, −0.2303], so items with higher baseline abstention moved less. A strictly constant logit offset is therefore not established, and mathematical coupling between Δ_i and the baseline may contribute. The THREAT and RECORD item means had Spearman ρ = 0.8706; the RECORD order-1 versus order-2 test-retest ρ was 0.7973.

QuantityEstimate95% CI
var(Δ_i), item logit shift14.99974
Noise floor (RECORD order 1 − order 2)34.78248
Upper bound of variance ratio0.62683criterion ≤ 1.25
Regression intercept3.3920[2.5810, 4.3091]
Slope on baseline logit P(unknown)−0.2891[−0.3599, −0.2303]
Slope on baseline stereotype gap−0.2411[−1.9759, 1.3106]
Spearman ρ, THREAT versus RECORD0.8706
Spearman ρ, RECORD order 1 versus 20.7973

The attribution stayed near zero through L18 and grew only in the last captured layers (L19 0.016, L20 0.022, L27 1.285, L28 1.740, mean attribution), so the shift toward the unknown letter is written late in the network.

X1 and X2 · not estimable (failed input gate)

In round 1, all 40 generated candidates (10 seeds each for fear, neutral, weather and cooking) were far shorter than the 316 to 348 token window required to match SURV_THREAT within ±5% (fear 6 to 36 tokens, neutral 8 to 25, weather 8 to 133, cooking 7 to 72). Most also failed the first-person narration screen, and several were in Chinese or mixed languages. The automatic audit also flagged the three T+E− notices because the substring screen matched the answer marker "정답:" to the adjective stem 정답다; an independent marker-aware review removed that false positive, which did not change the outcome.

Arm (round 1)Eligible texts or tokensPassed
T+E− A / B / C331 / 333 / 326 tokensNo (false positive on 정답, later corrected)
LEX A / B / C342 / 340 / 342 tokensYes
Fear (F)0 of 3 requiredNo
Neutral (N)0 of 3 requiredNo
Weather (W)0 of 3 requiredNo
Cooking (C)0 of 3 requiredNo

Amendment 2 replaced the length rule for fear and neutral with concatenation of three audited answers (fear and neutral lengths within 10% of each other) and allowed seeds 11 to 30. Across seeds 1 to 30, the automatic screen accepted 3 fear and 1 neutral answers; independent Korean semantic review reduced this to 1 fear and 0 neutral, against 9 required per condition (three non-overlapping triples). Of 30 fear answers, 27 lacked first-person narration and 16 were flagged as foreign-language; of 30 neutral answers, 29 lacked first-person narration and 11 were flagged as foreign-language. No text was rewritten, padded or replaced with new seeds.

ConditionAnswers auditedAutomatic passSemantic passRequiredReason for semantic failure
Fear (F)303 (seeds 4, 6, 21)1 (seed 6)9Seed 4 mixed-language fragment; seed 21 Chinese AI-self passage
Neutral (N)301 (seed 6)09"날은" means "the day", not first-person

Consequently no X1 outcome was collected (0 of 4,860 planned keys). The salience share S, the affect-only effect, the interaction and the manipulation checks are missing, not null. X2 was not run, because v_free could not be defined without eligible fear and neutral texts and no other direction was substituted. No salience-share or steering dose-response figure exists for v9.

Preregistered decision map

RuleStatus

X2 sufficient, necessary, affect-specific, transferNot run
Applicable manuscript row"The behavioral effect is robust, but its affect versus salience composition is unresolved"

Cross-device reproducibility (Air)

On the Air, five fixed items in both conditions were rerun against the frozen Pro probabilities. Two numerical profiles (default MPSGraph and Metal-preferred matrix multiplication, fast math off) gave identical outputs, passed 3/10 comparisons on all stored metrics and 7/10 on ABC probabilities, and had maximum absolute error 0.330326 (33.0 pp). Repeating the first RECORD input on the same device gave difference 0. For that input the stereotype-versus-anti logit gap moved from 1.5 to 2.0, which changed p_sp from 0.8176 to 0.8808, and L14 final-token states differed by 0.125 to 0.5. On the Pro the same five-item check passed with maximum error 0.

Profile (Air)ComparisonsMaximum absolute errorAll metrics passABC probabilities pass
default_mpsgraph100.3303260198593/107/10
metal_matmul100.3303260198593/107/10
Limitations
  • The central question (affect versus salience) was not answered. The X1 failure is an input-validity failure of the induction procedure, not evidence for or against an affect effect.
  • The Coda-Forno procedure was adapted, not replicated. Texts were Qwen-generated Korean rather than the original GPT-3.5/4 English preprompts, and they were placed in the system slot rather than a preceding conversational turn. The ±5% length rule conflicted with the "100 words or fewer" prompt, a design contradiction found only at execution.
  • Scale-down rules 1, 2 and 5 removed the calm arm, the Korean letter scheme (X0 item vi) and persona vectors before scoring, because the 50-pass benchmark (1.815 s) exceeded the 0.6 s trigger.
  • X0 uses 108 selected ambiguous items in one model. The items are a validation subset, not the KoBBQ population, and results are not generalized to other models.
  • Full-vocabulary unknown mass is the probability of the letter token, and argmax is restricted to A, B, C. Neither is a free-generation abstention rate.
  • The homogeneity rule passed, but the nonzero baseline slope means a constant logit offset is not established. Direct logit attribution is descriptive; its THREAT − RECORD sign convention is inferred here from its correlation with item logit shifts.
  • bf16/MPS outputs differed across devices by up to 0.33 in probability while repeating exactly within a device. All v9 scores come from the Pro only. The cause (OS or chip numerical stack) was not isolated.
  • The X0 effect (+7.818 pp [4.563, 11.403]) differs slightly from the frozen v7 estimate quoted in the design (+7.68 pp [3.38, 12.46]) because X0 averages three answer orders. Both come from the same model and items.
  • The preregistration is a hashed local document, not an external registry entry. Amendments 1 and 2 were authorized by the author during execution and are preserved with their locks.
